# Proposed OEIS comments: A282476 to A297723

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A282476

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282476>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [5038](../papers/5001-5500/5038-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 15*a(n-1) -60*a(n-2) +38*a(n-3) -417*a(n-4) +2211*a(n-5) +254*a(n-6) +9714*a(n-7) -19866*a(n-8) +11292*a(n-9) -83508*a(n-10) +94758*a(n-11) -162290*a(n-12) +333372*a(n-13) -354804*a(n-14) +698218*a(n-15) -723297*a(n-16) +960537*a(n-17) -1190332*a(n-18) +1011360*a(n-19) -1264665*a(n-20) +756661*a(n-21) -868110*a(n-22) +386904*a(n-23) -278456*a(n-24) +47328*a(n-25) -2976*a(n-26) +64*a(n-27)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 216 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 27. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A283039

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283039>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [203](../papers/0001-0500/0203-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 72 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 867 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 72, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A283723

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283723>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [183](../papers/0001-0500/0183-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 80 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 370 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 80, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: +126*n*(n-1)*(n+1)*a(n) -3*n*(n-1)*(691*n-1037)*a(n-1) +(n-1)*(12023*n^2-48049*n+47736)*a(n-2) +2*(-12889*n^3+93970*n^2-221427*n+169506)*a(n-3) +4*(-248*n^3+12718*n^2-88433*n+160113)*a(n-4) +16*(2918*n^3-41574*n^2+198532*n-317121)*a(n-5) +240*(4*n-19)*(2*n-9)*(4*n-21)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 25 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 5, so the recurrence holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [8196](../papers/8001-8500/8196-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 6*a(n-3) + 4*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + 4*x + 6*x^2 + 4*x^3 + 4*x^4) / (1 - x - 6*x^3 - 4*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 22 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A296112

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A296112>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [155](../papers/0001-0500/0155-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 89 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 056 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 89, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [7588](../papers/7501-8000/7588-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 12*a(n-3) + 2*a(n-4) + 2*a(n-5) - 7*a(n-6) - a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + 11*x + 14*x^2 + 4*x^3 - 5*x^4 - 8*x^5 - x^6) / (1 - x - 12*x^3 - 2*x^4 - 2*x^5 + 7*x^6 + x^7). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 28 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [8201](../papers/8001-8500/8201-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) + 9*a(n-3) - 6*a(n-4) - 8*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + 7*x + 3*x^2 - 14*x^3 - 8*x^4) / (1 - 3*x + 2*x^2 - 9*x^3 + 6*x^4 + 8*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 28 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
